========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Hello, Doctor. Thank you so much for seeing me today. I... well, I hope I'm not taking up too much of your time. 

It's just... some folks at church suggested I come talk to you. I guess I've been doing some... oh, just some slightly silly things. Nothing bad, I promise! Just... well, like at the potluck table. Sometimes I'll just be standing there helping, and I guess I'll hold the serving spoon up in the air and... and I just freeze. I don't move at all for a bit. Or I'll be trying to help set up, and I just keep folding the very same stack of napkins, over and over, even while people are standing there waiting on me. 

It's just a little embarrassing, really. I don't want to be a bother to anyone or make them think poorly of me. Am I explaining that okay for you, Doctor? I hope that's the kind of thing you need to know.

Oh, my, no. I... I don't remember having any trouble like that at all. I was always a very good, quiet girl in the classroom. 

Now, I didn't stay in school past the eighth grade, but... well, I promise that wasn't because I couldn't learn! It was just... you know, back in those days, a lot of us girls left school early to help our mothers with the house and the younger children. I picked up all my homemaking skills just fine, and I always did as I was told and tried my very best with the reading and writing lessons I did have. 

I hope it's alright with you that I don't have a lot of fancy schooling, Doctor? I've always been a hard worker, I promise. Does that answer what you were looking for?

Oh, well... strict? No, I wouldn't say strict, exactly. I mean, I do have my little habits, like having my tea in the morning and doing my dusting, but... well, I'm certainly not one of those fussy people who has to do everything at the exact same minute every day!

Or I'll be trying to help set up, and I just keep folding the very same stack of napkins, over and over, even while people are standing there waiting on me. \n\nIt's just a little embarrassing, really. I don't want to be a bother to anyone or make them think poorly of me. Am I explaining that okay for you, Doctor? I hope that's the kind of thing you need to know.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel upset or distressed when you find yourself freezing or repeating actions like folding napkins?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no, I wouldn't say I feel distressed. I hope that's alright to say. To be perfectly honest, I don't really feel much of anything when it happens. It's just... well, my mind just goes a little quiet for a minute, I suppose. I don't even really realize I'm stuck like that until one of the ladies gently nudges my arm. \n\nI certainly don't feel upset inside, I promise! The only thing that bothers me is just... worrying I might be in someone's way or making a bad impression at the church. I just want to be helpful, you know? Does that make sense, Doctor? I hope I'm answering your question right.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any thoughts that keep repeating in your mind?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. I... I don't think I have anything like that. My mind is usually just... well, it's pretty peaceful, really. I mostly just think about my daily chores around the house, or what I need to bake for the next church gathering. \n\nNothing really gets stuck in there repeating, I promise! I mean, I don't get out a whole lot besides going to services, but that's just because I like being home. I'm a bit of a homebody, I suppose! \n\nI have my nice neighbors, and... well, we wave when we get the mail. I don't really need a big, busy social calendar at my age anyway. Oh, an attachment to objects? Like... carrying a blanket around, or something like that? Oh, no, definitely not. I was much too sensible for that, even when I was little. 

I mean, I had a little doll, of course—just a normal rag doll that my auntie made for me. But I didn't fuss over it or drag it everywhere I went. I just kept it neatly on my bed, where it belonged. I wouldn't have dreamed of making a fuss over a toy. I was always very mature for my age, I think. 

I just... I never wanted to seem silly or attached to things like a baby would be. I hope that makes sense? I'm trying very hard to give you the clear answers you need, Doctor. Am I doing okay with these questions?

Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says.   },

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Unable to stop? Oh, my, no. I've never been out of control like that. I've always prided myself on having good manners and... well, self-restraint, I suppose you'd call it. 

I know some folks struggle with things like smoking or... or other bad habits they just can't quit, but I've never had anything like that. If I need to stop doing something, I just stop. It's just a matter of willpower, isn't it? I'd be so embarrassed if I couldn't control myself. I've always tried to be very proper and well-behaved.

I hope that's a good answer? I want to make sure I'm giving you all the right information so you can do your job properly. Am I doing alright?

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Stuck? Oh... well... oh dear. I suppose I should just tell you, though it's dreadfully embarrassing. I always try to be so helpful, you know, but... well, I actually came to see you because I've been doing some odd little things at church lately. 

Just silly things, really, nothing malicious! Like... at the potluck table, I found myself just standing there, holding the serving spoon up in the air, and I just wasn't moving. And then another time, I was folding the exact same stack of napkins over and over again, just smoothing them out, even with people waiting on me in line to get one. 

I felt so foolish when I realized I was holding everyone up. I certainly didn't mean to be a bother to anyone, I promise. I just sort of... got stuck on it, like you said. Is that... is that the sort of thing you meant, Doctor? I hope I haven't painted a bad picture of myself. I really do try to be a good helper.

Oh, goodness. I've always loved visiting with folks. I try to be good company and polite to everyone, I really do. But... well, I suppose I should be honest with you, even if it makes me sound a bit silly. I don't want you to think poorly of me, but...

I don't want to be a bother to anyone or make them think poorly of me. Am I explaining that okay for you, Doctor? I hope that's the kind of thing you need to know.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel upset or distressed when you find yourself freezing or repeating actions like folding napkins?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no, I wouldn't say I feel distressed. I hope that's alright to say. To be perfectly honest, I don't really feel much of anything when it happens. It's just... well, my mind just goes a little quiet for a minute, I suppose. I don't even really realize I'm stuck like that until one of the ladies gently nudges my arm. \n\nI certainly don't feel upset inside, I promise! The only thing that bothers me is just... worrying I might be in someone's way or making a bad impression at the church. I just want to be helpful, you know? Does that make sense, Doctor? I hope I'm answering your question right.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any thoughts that keep repeating in your mind?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. I... I don't think I have anything like that. My mind is usually just... well, it's pretty peaceful, really. I mostly just think about my daily chores around the house, or what I need to bake for the next church gathering. \n\nNothing really gets stuck in there repeating, I promise! I hope that's a good thing? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of answers, Doctor. Am I doing okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you have difficulty making friends or connecting with others outside of church?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... no, I wouldn't say I have difficulty, exactly. I mean, I don't get out a whole lot besides going to services, but that's just because I like being home. I'm a bit of a homebody, I suppose! \n\nI have my nice neighbors, and... well, we wave when we get the mail. I don't really need a big, busy social calendar at my age anyway. I'm perfectly happy just keeping to myself and helping out when the church needs me. That's perfectly normal for someone my age, isn't it, Doctor? I hope it doesn't sound like I'm unfriendly or anything like that. I'm always polite to everyone I meet, I promise.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any hobbies or activities that you enjoy doing in your free time?\n\nPatient: Oh, my... hobbies. Well, I suppose I don't do anything too fancy. Mostly I just... well, I like keeping my little house neat as a pin. Sweeping up, dusting the knick-knacks, making sure everything is in its place. It keeps me busy enough! And sometimes I just like to sit quietly and listen to the radio in the afternoons. \n\nI don't really need a lot of excitement, I'm afraid. I hope that doesn't make me sound too boring or lazy, Doctor? I just think at my age, it's perfectly alright to take things a little easy and enjoy the quiet. Is that an okay answer for your notes? I just want to make sure I'm being helpful to you.\n\nDoctor: Did you have any difficulties with learning or developing skills as a child?\n\nPatient: Oh, my, no. I... I don't remember having any trouble like that at all. I was always a very good, quiet girl in the classroom. \n\nNow, I didn't stay in school past the eighth grade, but... well, I promise that wasn't because I couldn't learn! It was just... you know, back in those days, a lot of us girls left school early to help our mothers with the house and the younger children. I picked up all my homemaking skills just fine, and I always did as I was told and tried my very best with the reading and writing lessons I did have. \n\nI hope it's alright with you that I don't have a lot of fancy schooling, Doctor? I've always been a hard worker, I promise. Does that answer what you were looking for?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself following a very strict daily routine?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... strict? No, I wouldn't say strict, exactly. I mean, I do have my little habits, like having my tea in the morning and doing my dusting, but... well, I'm certainly not one of those fussy people who has to do everything at the exact same minute every day! \n\nI just sort of take the day as it comes, mostly. If I don't get to sweeping until the afternoon, well, that's perfectly fine by me. I promise I'm not set in my ways or... or stubborn like some older folks can get. I try to be very agreeable and just go with the flow. \n\nI hope it doesn't sound like I'm too rigid, Doctor? I just like keeping a nice, peaceful home. Is that an alright way to do things? I really do want to make sure I'm giving you the right sort of answers.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any strong, persistent interests that seem unusual or overly focused to others?\n\nPatient: Oh, my... unusual interests? Goodness, no. I... I don't think so, anyway. I've always just been perfectly happy with my normal, everyday things. I don't get all wrapped up in... well, anything strange or out of the ordinary, I promise. \n\nLike I said, just keeping the house nice and listening to my programs is plenty for me. I hope it's not a bad thing that I don't have any big, intense passions? I just always thought it was best to be sensible and not make a fuss about things. Is... is that the right kind of answer for your notes, Doctor? I just really want to make sure I'm doing a good job for you.\n\nDoctor: Did you have any behaviors as a child that you repeated over and over, like hand flapping or rocking?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. I... I never did anything like that. Rocking or flapping my hands... oh my, that sounds like something a troubled child might do, doesn't it? I was always very still and well-behaved, I promise. \n\nMy mother would have had a fit if I was carrying on, waving my hands about or making a spectacle of myself. I just sat quietly with my hands folded in my lap, just like a good girl should. I didn't want to be any bother to anyone, you see. \n\nI hope that's alright to say? I just... I want you to know I was a very normal, polite little girl. I didn't cause any trouble at all. Is that what you were hoping to hear, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Did you ever have any strong attachment to specific objects as a child?\n\nPatient: Oh, an attachment to objects? Like... carrying a blanket around, or something like that? Oh, no, definitely not. I was much too sensible for that, even when I was little. \n\nI mean, I had a little doll, of course—just a normal rag doll that my auntie made for me. But I didn't fuss over it or drag it everywhere I went. I just kept it neatly on my bed, where it belonged. I wouldn't have dreamed of making a fuss over a toy. I was always very mature for my age, I think. \n\nI just... I never wanted to seem silly or attached to things like a baby would be. I hope that makes sense? I'm trying very hard to give you the clear answers you need, Doctor. Am I doing okay with these questions?\n\nDoctor: Did you ever feel like you had to follow certain rules or rituals in your daily life to feel comfortable or safe?\n\nPatient: Rules or rituals? Oh, dear, no. I've never been one of those people who has to touch the doorknob three times or anything like that. That sounds so exhausting, doesn't it? \n\nI just... well, I like things tidy and I have my routine, but that's just being organized, isn't it? I get up, I make my tea, I listen to the radio... it's just a normal day. I don't feel like I *have* to do anything to feel safe, I just do what needs to be done. I've always been very easygoing, really. \n\nI don't have any strange habits like that, I promise. I just try to live a quiet, normal life. Is that the sort of thing you're looking for, Doctor? I hope my answers are helpful for you.\n\nDoctor: Did you ever feel like you had a strong preference for doing things in a very specific way?\n\nPatient: A specific way? Oh, no... I don't think so. I mean, I like to do things properly, of course, like keeping a nice home and all, but I'm not... I'm not stubborn about it. \n\nI know some people get terribly upset if you change how things are done, but I've always tried to just go along with whatever other people want. It's much nicer to be agreeable, don't you think? I never wanted to be one of those difficult ladies who insists on having everything exactly her way. I just try to be flexible and stay out of the way, really. \n\nDoes that sound alright? I'm trying my best to think back, and I just hope I'm giving you the right kind of answers, Doctor.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to do things in a certain order or sequence to feel comfortable?\n\nPatient: In a certain order? Oh, goodness, no. I mean... I suppose everyone does things in a sensible way, like putting your socks on before your shoes, but I don't have a... a strict sequence I have to follow or anything. \n\nI wouldn't want to be rigid like that. It's much better to just take things as they come, isn't it? I'd hate for anyone to think I was fussy or demanding about how my day goes. I just try to be easy to get along with, no matter what happens. \n\nAm I... am I giving you what you need, Doctor? I do hope I'm being helpful with all these questions.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you were unable to stop a behavior or action even when you wanted to?\n\nPatient: Unable to stop? Oh, my, no. I've never been out of control like that. I've always prided myself on having good manners and... well, self-restraint, I suppose you'd call it. \n\nI know some folks struggle with things like smoking or... or other bad habits they just can't quit, but I've never had anything like that. If I need to stop doing something, I just stop. It's just a matter of willpower, isn't it? I'd be so embarrassed if I couldn't control myself. I've always tried to be very proper and well-behaved.\n\nI hope that's a good answer? I want to make sure I'm giving you all the right information so you can do your job properly. Am I doing alright?\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you often get stuck on doing the same things over and over, even when you're not necessarily enjoying them?\n\nPatient: Stuck? Oh... well... oh dear. I suppose I should just tell you, though it's dreadfully embarrassing. I always try to be so helpful, you know, but... well, I actually came to see you because I've been doing some odd little things at church lately. \n\nJust silly things, really, nothing malicious! Like... at the potluck table, I found myself just standing there, holding the serving spoon up in the air, and I just wasn't moving. And then another time, I was folding the exact same stack of napkins over and over again, just smoothing them out, even with people waiting on me in line to get one. \n\nI felt so foolish when I realized I was holding everyone up. I certainly didn't mean to be a bother to anyone, I promise. I just sort of... got stuck on it, like you said. Is that... is that the sort of thing you meant, Doctor? I hope I haven't painted a bad picture of myself. I really do try to be a good helper.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find it hard to start or maintain conversations with others?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness. I've always loved visiting with folks. I try to be good company and polite to everyone, I really do. But... well, I suppose I should be honest with you, even if it makes me sound a bit silly. I don't want you to think poorly of me, but... \n\nSometimes lately, when the ladies ask me something simple, like about the blankets or choir practice, I truly mean to answer them straight. I want to give a helpful answer! But my words just sort of... run off. I'll start talking about canning beans, and then the hymns we sang, and then how my roses are doing out front... all jumbled together, it seems. And the poor dears, they just look at me like they can't find what I'm saying at all. \n\nI feel so awful when I mix people up like that. I certainly don't mean to be confusing or make conversations difficult. Am I... am I making sense to you now, Doctor? I hope this is the kind of answer you were looking for, I just want to make sure I'm doing a good job for you.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself avoiding social gatherings or events because you feel uncomfortable around other people?\n\nPatient: Oh, I usually just love our church gatherings. I've always enjoyed being around folks, really, and I wouldn't want you to think I'm antisocial or anything like that. I've always prided myself on being involved. \n\nBut... well... oh dear, it's a bit hard to admit. I suppose I have been staying away a little bit lately. I've actually had to... step back from some of my volunteering with the choir and the church groups. It's just that I... I kept mixing up the plans, you see. And I could tell it was making the other ladies uncomfortable, having to quietly go behind me and redo the things I was supposed to handle. \n\nI felt just terrible about it. I only ever want to be a help to them, never a burden, so I thought it was best to just bow out for now so I don't ruin their hard work. I hope that makes sense? I do try my best, Doctor, I really do. I hope you don't think I'm just being lazy... did I answer that alright for you?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. The only thing that bothers me is just... worrying I might be in someone's way or making a bad impression at the church. I just want to be helpful, you know? Does that make sense, Doctor? I hope I'm answering your question right.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any thoughts that keep repeating in your mind?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. I... I don't think I have anything like that. My mind is usually just... well, it's pretty peaceful, really. I mostly just think about my daily chores around the house, or what I need to bake for the next church gathering. \n\nNothing really gets stuck in there repeating, I promise! I hope that's a good thing? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of answers, Doctor. Am I doing okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you have difficulty making friends or connecting with others outside of church?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... no, I wouldn't say I have difficulty, exactly. I mean, I don't get out a whole lot besides going to services, but that's just because I like being home. I'm a bit of a homebody, I suppose! \n\nI have my nice neighbors, and... well, we wave when we get the mail. I don't really need a big, busy social calendar at my age anyway. I'm perfectly happy just keeping to myself and helping out when the church needs me. That's perfectly normal for someone my age, isn't it, Doctor? I hope it doesn't sound like I'm unfriendly or anything like that. I'm always polite to everyone I meet, I promise.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any hobbies or activities that you enjoy doing in your free time?\n\nPatient: Oh, my... hobbies. I hope it doesn't sound like I'm unfriendly or anything like that. I'm always polite to everyone I meet, I promise.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any hobbies or activities that you enjoy doing in your free time?\n\nPatient: Oh, my... hobbies. Well, I suppose I don't do anything too fancy. Mostly I just... well, I like keeping my little house neat as a pin. Sweeping up, dusting the knick-knacks, making sure everything is in its place. It keeps me busy enough! And sometimes I just like to sit quietly and listen to the radio in the afternoons. \n\nI don't really need a lot of excitement, I'm afraid. I hope that doesn't make me sound too boring or lazy, Doctor? I just think at my age, it's perfectly alright to take things a little easy and enjoy the quiet. Is that an okay answer for your notes? I just want to make sure I'm being helpful to you.\n\nDoctor: Did you have any difficulties with learning or developing skills as a child?\n\nPatient: Oh, my, no. I... I don't remember having any trouble like that at all. I was always a very good, quiet girl in the classroom. \n\nNow, I didn't stay in school past the eighth grade, but... well, I promise that wasn't because I couldn't learn! It was just... you know, back in those days, a lot of us girls left school early to help our mothers with the house and the younger children. I picked up all my homemaking skills just fine, and I always did as I was told and tried my very best with the reading and writing lessons I did have. \n\nI hope it's alright with you that I don't have a lot of fancy schooling, Doctor? I've always been a hard worker, I promise. Does that answer what you were looking for?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself following a very strict daily routine?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... strict? No, I wouldn't say strict, exactly. I mean, I do have my little habits, like having my tea in the morning and doing my dusting, but... well, I'm certainly not one of those fussy people who has to do everything at the exact same minute every day! \n\nI just sort of take the day as it comes, mostly. If I don't get to sweeping until the afternoon, well, that's perfectly fine by me. I promise I'm not set in my ways or... or stubborn like some older folks can get. I try to be very agreeable and just go with the flow. \n\nI hope it doesn't sound like I'm too rigid, Doctor? I just like keeping a nice, peaceful home. Is that an alright way to do things? I really do want to make sure I'm giving you the right sort of answers.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any strong, persistent interests that seem unusual or overly focused to others?\n\nPatient: Oh, my... unusual interests? Goodness, no. I... I don't think so, anyway. I've always just been perfectly happy with my normal, everyday things. I don't get all wrapped up in... well, anything strange or out of the ordinary, I promise. \n\nLike I said, just keeping the house nice and listening to my programs is plenty for me. I hope it's not a bad thing that I don't have any big, intense passions? I just always thought it was best to be sensible and not make a fuss about things. Is... is that the right kind of answer for your notes, Doctor? I just really want to make sure I'm doing a good job for you.\n\nDoctor: Did you have any behaviors as a child that you repeated over and over, like hand flapping or rocking?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. I... I never did anything like that. Rocking or flapping my hands... oh my, that sounds like something a troubled child might do, doesn't it? I was always very still and well-behaved, I promise. \n\nMy mother would have had a fit if I was carrying on, waving my hands about or making a spectacle of myself. I just sat quietly with my hands folded in my lap, just like a good girl should. I didn't want to be any bother to anyone, you see. \n\nI hope that's alright to say? I just... I want you to know I was a very normal, polite little girl. I didn't cause any trouble at all. Is that what you were hoping to hear, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Did you ever have any strong attachment to specific objects as a child?\n\nPatient: Oh, an attachment to objects? Like... carrying a blanket around, or something like that? Oh, no, definitely not. I was much too sensible for that, even when I was little. \n\nI mean, I had a little doll, of course—just a normal rag doll that my auntie made for me. But I didn't fuss over it or drag it everywhere I went. I just kept it neatly on my bed, where it belonged. I wouldn't have dreamed of making a fuss over a toy. I was always very mature for my age, I think. \n\nI just... I never wanted to seem silly or attached to things like a baby would be. I hope that makes sense? I'm trying very hard to give you the clear answers you need, Doctor. Am I doing okay with these questions?\n\nDoctor: Did you ever feel like you had to follow certain rules or rituals in your daily life to feel comfortable or safe?\n\nPatient: Rules or rituals? Oh, dear, no. I've never been one of those people who has to touch the doorknob three times or anything like that. That sounds so exhausting, doesn't it? \n\nI just... well, I like things tidy and I have my routine, but that's just being organized, isn't it? I get up, I make my tea, I listen to the radio... it's just a normal day. I don't feel like I *have* to do anything to feel safe, I just do what needs to be done. I've always been very easygoing, really. \n\nI don't have any strange habits like that, I promise. I just try to live a quiet, normal life. Is that the sort of thing you're looking for, Doctor? I hope my answers are helpful for you.\n\nDoctor: Did you ever feel like you had a strong preference for doing things in a very specific way?\n\nPatient: A specific way? Oh, no... I don't think so. I mean, I like to do things properly, of course, like keeping a nice home and all, but I'm not... I'm not stubborn about it. \n\nI know some people get terribly upset if you change how things are done, but I've always tried to just go along with whatever other people want. It's much nicer to be agreeable, don't you think? I never wanted to be one of those difficult ladies who insists on having everything exactly her way. I just try to be flexible and stay out of the way, really. \n\nDoes that sound alright? I'm trying my best to think back, and I just hope I'm giving you the right kind of answers, Doctor.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to do things in a certain order or sequence to feel comfortable?\n\nPatient: In a certain order? Oh, goodness, no. I mean... I suppose everyone does things in a sensible way, like putting your socks on before your shoes, but I don't have a... a strict sequence I have to follow or anything. \n\nI wouldn't want to be rigid like that. It's much better to just take things as they come, isn't it? I'd hate for anyone to think I was fussy or demanding about how my day goes. I just try to be easy to get along with, no matter what happens. \n\nAm I... am I giving you what you need, Doctor? I do hope I'm being helpful with all these questions.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you were unable to stop a behavior or action even when you wanted to?\n\nPatient: Unable to stop? Oh, my, no. I've never been out of control like that. I've always prided myself on having good manners and... well, self-restraint, I suppose you'd call it. \n\nI know some folks struggle with things like smoking or... or other bad habits they just can't quit, but I've never had anything like that. If I need to stop doing something, I just stop. It's just a matter of willpower, isn't it? I'd be so embarrassed if I couldn't control myself. I've always tried to be very proper and well-behaved.\n\nI hope that's a good answer? I want to make sure I'm giving you all the right information so you can do your job properly. Am I doing alright?\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you often get stuck on doing the same things over and over, even when you're not necessarily enjoying them?\n\nPatient: Stuck? Oh... well... oh dear. I suppose I should just tell you, though it's dreadfully embarrassing. I always try to be so helpful, you know, but... well, I actually came to see you because I've been doing some odd little things at church lately. \n\nJust silly things, really, nothing malicious! Like... at the potluck table, I found myself just standing there, holding the serving spoon up in the air, and I just wasn't moving. And then another time, I was folding the exact same stack of napkins over and over again, just smoothing them out, even with people waiting on me in line to get one. \n\nI felt so foolish when I realized I was holding everyone up. I certainly didn't mean to be a bother to anyone, I promise. I just sort of... got stuck on it, like you said. Is that... is that the sort of thing you meant, Doctor? I hope I haven't painted a bad picture of myself. I really do try to be a good helper.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find it hard to start or maintain conversations with others?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness. I've always loved visiting with folks. I try to be good company and polite to everyone, I really do. But... well, I suppose I should be honest with you, even if it makes me sound a bit silly. I don't want you to think poorly of me, but... \n\nSometimes lately, when the ladies ask me something simple, like about the blankets or choir practice, I truly mean to answer them straight. I want to give a helpful answer! But my words just sort of... run off. I'll start talking about canning beans, and then the hymns we sang, and then how my roses are doing out front... all jumbled together, it seems. And the poor dears, they just look at me like they can't find what I'm saying at all. \n\nI feel so awful when I mix people up like that. I certainly don't mean to be confusing or make conversations difficult. Am I... am I making sense to you now, Doctor? I hope this is the kind of answer you were looking for, I just want to make sure I'm doing a good job for you.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself avoiding social gatherings or events because you feel uncomfortable around other people?\n\nPatient: Oh, I usually just love our church gatherings. I've always enjoyed being around folks, really, and I wouldn't want you to think I'm antisocial or anything like that. I've always prided myself on being involved. \n\nBut... well... oh dear, it's a bit hard to admit. I suppose I have been staying away a little bit lately. I've actually had to... step back from some of my volunteering with the choir and the church groups. It's just that I... I kept mixing up the plans, you see. And I could tell it was making the other ladies uncomfortable, having to quietly go behind me and redo the things I was supposed to handle. \n\nI felt just terrible about it. I only ever want to be a help to them, never a burden, so I thought it was best to just bow out for now so I don't ruin their hard work. I hope that makes sense? I do try my best, Doctor, I really do. I hope you don't think I'm just being lazy... did I answer that alright for you?\n\nDoctor: Did you ever have any behaviors or interests as a child that you felt compelled to repeat or engage in excessively?\n\nPatient: Oh... as a child? Goodness, that was a long time ago. I... I don't think so. I was always a very good little girl, very obedient. I helped my mother around the house and did my chores just like I was supposed to. \n\nI don't recall ever doing anything... well, excessive or strange like that. I just played with my dolls and helped shell peas on the porch, very normal things. I wouldn't want you to think I was a difficult child or anything. I always tried to be so good and not cause any trouble for my parents. \n\nDoes that... does that sound alright to you, Doctor? I hope I'm remembering things the way you need me to. I really want to be helpful.\n\nDoctor: Did you have any favorite toys or activities as a child that you played with repeatedly for long periods of time?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I suppose I had my favorites, just like any little girl would. I had a sweet little doll my auntie made for me, and I loved playing house with my sisters. But... no, I wouldn't say I played with anything over and over for hours on end. \n\nI was always very mindful of when it was time to put my things away and help my mother with supper or whatever she needed. I never wanted to be a bother or get too wrapped up in my own little world, you know? I just did the regular, everyday things. \n\nI hope I'm not disappointing you by not having a more interesting answer, Doctor. I just always tried to do exactly what was expected of me. Is it alright that I just played normally like that?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }
